2025-06-17T23:01:49.4025338Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4025751Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4026246Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4026380Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4026490Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4027387Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4028232Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4028336Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4029018Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4029515Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4029845Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4030180Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4030511Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4030840Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4031309Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4031482Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4032209Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4032898Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4033001Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4033140Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4033270Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4033703Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4033975Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4034380Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4035510Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4036204Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4036527Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4036637Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4036942Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4037137Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4037240Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4037468Z ```bash
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4038004Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4038614Z ```
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4038719Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4038814Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4039055Z ```bash
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4039752Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4040512Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4040746Z ```
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4040858Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4041017Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4041267Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4041371Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4041973Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4042501Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4042589Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4042853Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4043351Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4043877Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4044366Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4045052Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4045381Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4045767Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4046263Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4046354Z Fixes #11.

2025-06-17T23:01:49.4057536Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4058022Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4058140Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4058346Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4058438Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4058936Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4059481Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4060046Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4060603Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4060954Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4061445Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4061878Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4061972Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4062261Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4062606Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4062921Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4063128Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4063230Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4064222Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4065233Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4065317Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4066017Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4066696Z

2025-06-17T23:01:49.4071967Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4072250Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4072636Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4072726Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4072887Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4073109Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4073438Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4073513Z ```json
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4073690Z {
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4073870Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4074101Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4074371Z   },
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4074550Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4074946Z     {
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4075190Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4075428Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4075649Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4075852Z     }
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076023Z   ]
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076196Z }
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076362Z ```
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076461Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076547Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076691Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4076982Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4077517Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4078037Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4078518Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4078663Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4078763Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4078927Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4079395Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4079956Z 
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4080176Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4080652Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4081107Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T23:01:49.4081417Z

2025-06-17T23:08:51.7825446Z               - heading "Navigate to header Mini-history of Prefect" [level=2] [ref=e218]:
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7826051Z                 - link "Navigate to header" [ref=e220] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7826607Z                   - /url: "#mini-history-of-prefect"
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7826982Z                   - img [ref=e222] [cursor=pointer]
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7827546Z                 - generic [ref=e224] [cursor=pointer]: Mini-history of Prefect
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7828061Z               - paragraph [ref=e225]:
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7828428Z                 - strong [ref=e226]: "2018-2021:"
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7831236Z                 - text: Our story begins in 2018, when we introduced the idea that workflow orchestration should be Pythonic. Inspired by distributed tools like Dask, and building on the experience of our founder, Jeremiah Lowin (a PMC member of Apache Airflow), we created a system based on simple Python decorators for tasks and flows. But what made Prefect truly special was our introduction of task mapping—a feature that would later become foundational to our dynamic execution capabilities (and eventually imitated by other orchestration SDKs).
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7833931Z               - paragraph [ref=e227]:
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7834296Z                 - strong [ref=e228]: "2022:"
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7837066Z                 - text: "Prefect’s 2.0 release became inevitable once we recognized that real-world workflows don’t always fit into neat, pre-planned DAG structures: sometimes you need to update a job definition based on runtime information, for example by skipping a branch of your workflow. So we removed a key constraint that workflows be written explicitly as DAGs, fully embracing native Python control flow—if/else conditionals, while loops-everything that makes Python…Python."
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7839328Z               - paragraph [ref=e229]:
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7839768Z                 - strong [ref=e230]: "2023-present:"
2025-06-17T23:08:51.7841607Z                 - text: With our release of Prefect 3.0 in 2024, we fully embraced these dynamic patterns by open-sourcing our events and automations backend, allowing users to natively represent event-driven workflows and gain additional observability into their execution. Prefect 3.0 also unlocked a leap forward in performance, improving the runtime overhead of Prefect by up to 90%.

2025-06-17T23:21:11.9028305Z           return f"Processed {customer_id}"
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9028620Z       
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9028804Z       @flow
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9029031Z       def main():
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9029300Z           customer_ids = get_customer_ids()
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9029677Z           results = process_customer.map(customer_ids)
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9030033Z           return results
2025-06-17T23:21:11.9030279Z       ```
